\mathbb{C}\setminus\{\dots,-2,-1,0\}
.

Other important identities we use in our paper are the
q
-binomial theorems
. To state them, we need to define the
q
-Pochhammer symbols

(x;q)_{n}
and
(x;q)_{\infty}
, for any
x\in\mathbb{C}
and
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
by

\displaystyle(x;q)_{n}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny def}}}}{{=}}}\begin{cases}1&\textrm{if }n=0,\\
\displaystyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{n}{\big(1-xq^{i-1}\big)}&\textrm{if }n\geq 1,\\
\displaystyle\prod\limits_{i=1}^{\infty}{\big(1-xq^{i-1}\big)}&\textrm{if }n=\infty.\end{cases}

Note that
|q|<1
implies that the product defining
(x;q)_{\infty}
is uniformly convergent for
x\in\mathbb{C}
, and thus
(x;q)_{\infty}
is an entire function.

The
q
-binomial formula is the following

Theorem A.3

(
[
2
, Theorem 10.2.1]
)
.

For
|z|<1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}{\frac{(a;q)_{n}}{(q;q)_{n}}z^{n}}=\frac{(az;q)_{\infty}}{(z;q)_{\infty}}

Corollary A.4
.

For
z\in\mathbb{C}
,
m\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle\sum_{n=0}^{M}{\frac{(q^{-1};q^{-1})_{M}}{(q^{-1};q^{-1})_{n}(q^{-1};q^{-1})_{M-n}}(-1)^{n}q^{-{n\choose 2}}z^{n}}=\big(z;q^{-1}\big)_{M}.

Proof.

Let
a=q^{-M}
in Theorem
A.3
, and use
(q;q)_{n}=(-1)^{n}q^{{n+1\choose 2}}(q^{-1};q^{-1})_{n}
. The statement is then proved for any
|z|<1
; therefore it also holds for any
z\in\mathbb{C}
because both sides are polynomials on
z
.
∎

Another application of the
q
-binomial theorem is the following limit.

Theorem A.5

(
[
2
, Theorem 10.2.4]
)
.

For any
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
such that
b-a\notin\mathbb{Z}
, the following limit

\displaystyle\lim_{q\rightarrow 1}{\frac{(xq^{a};q)_{\infty}}{(xq^{b};q)_{\infty}}}=(1-x)^{b-a}

holds uniformly on compact subsets of
\{x\in\mathbb{C}\colon|x|\leq 1,x\neq 1\}
.

Remark A.6
.

If
b-a\in\mathbb{Z}
, the limit in Theorem
A.5
holds uniformly on compact subsets of
\mathbb{C}\setminus\{1\}
.

Appendix B
Some properties of the rational functions
\boldsymbol{C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})}

Lemma B.1
.

Assume
t=q^{\theta}
, for some
\theta\in\mathbb{N}
. Let
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
be
n
variables, then
C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=0
if some of the integers
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}
is strictly larger than
\theta
.

Proof.

Let us first rewrite the expression for
C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})
, as it was done in
[
30
, Section 6]
. Let
v_{i}=q^{\tau_{i}}u_{i}
for
i=1,\dots,n
. Also, given
\tau=(\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n})
, let
T=T_{\tau}\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny def}}}}{{=}}}\{k\in\{1,2,\dots,n\}\colon\tau_{k}\neq 0\}
. Then

\displaystyle C^{(q,t)}_{\tau}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=\prod_{k\in T}{t^{\tau_{k}-1}\frac{(q/t;q)_{\tau_{k}-1}}{(q;q)_{\tau_{k}-1}}\frac{(qu_{k};q)_{\tau_{k}}}{(qtu_{k};q)_{\tau_{k}}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{C^{(q, t)}_{\tau}(u_1, \ldots, u_n) =}{}\times\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}{\frac{(qu_{i}/tu_{j};q)_{\tau_{i}}}{(qu_{i}/u_{j};q)_{\tau_{i}}}\frac{(tu_{i}/v_{j};q)_{\tau_{i}}}{(u_{i}/v_{j};q)_{\tau_{i}}}}F_{\tau}(u;q,t),

(B.1)

where

\displaystyle F_{\tau}(u;q,t)\mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny def}}}}{{=}}}\sum_{K\subset T}(-1)^{|K|}(1/t)^{{|K|\choose 2}}\prod_{j\in T-K}{\frac{t-q^{\tau_{j}}}{1-q^{\tau_{j}}}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{F_{\tau}(u; q, t) \mathrel{\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\makebox[0.0pt]{\mbox{\tiny def}}}}{{=}}}}{}\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in K\\
j\in T-K\end{subarray}}{\frac{v_{j}-v_{k}/t}{v_{j}-v_{k}}}\prod_{k\in K}{\left(\frac{1-tv_{k}}{1-v_{k}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in T\\
i\neq k\end{subarray}}{\frac{u_{i}-v_{k}}{u_{i}-v_{k}/t}}\right)}.

Due to the factor
\prod\limits_{k\in T}{(q/t;q)_{\tau_{k}-1}}
in (
B.1
), it follows that
\tau_{k}-\theta\in\mathbb{N}=\{1,2,\dots\}
, for some
k
, implies
C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=0
, as desired.
∎

Lemma B.2

(
[
30
, Lemma 6.1]
)
.

Assume
t=q
. Let
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
and
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
be
n
variables, then
C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})=0
if some of the integers
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}
is strictly larger than
1
. If all
\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}\in\{0,1\}
, then
C^{(q,t)}_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}(u_{1},\dots,u_{n})
does not depend on the variables
u_{1},\dots,u_{n}
and

\displaystyle C_{\tau_{1},\dots,\tau_{n}}^{(q,t)}=(-1)^{\tau_{1}+\dots+\tau_{n}}.

Lemma B.3
.

Assume
\theta\in\mathbb{N}
. If we let
a_{n}^{(\theta)}=\frac{C_{n}^{(q,q^{\theta})}(x_{2}^{-1}x_{1}q^{-\theta})}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\theta-1}{(x_{1}-q^{i}x_{2})}}
, for all
0\leq n\leq\theta
, then these expressions satisfy the relations

\displaystyle a_{n}^{(\theta)}=\frac{1}{x_{1}-x_{2}}\big(T_{q,x_{2}}a_{n}^{(\theta-1)}-T_{q,x_{1}}a_{n-1}^{(\theta-1)}\big),\qquad 1\leq n\leq\theta-1,

\displaystyle a_{0}^{(\theta)}=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\theta-1}{(x_{1}-q^{i}x_{2})}},\qquad a_{\theta}^{(\theta)}=\frac{1}{\prod\limits_{i=0}^{\theta-1}{(x_{2}-q^{i}x_{1})}}.

Proof.

The expression
C_{n}^{(q,t)}(u)
, for
n\in\mathbb{Z}_{\geq 0}
, is much simpler than the general expression (
2.5
). It was first found by Jing and Joźefiak in
[
26
]
and it reads
C_{n}^{(q,t)}(u)=t^{n}\frac{(1/t;q)_{n}}{(q;q)_{n}}\frac{(u;q)_{n}}{(qtu;q)_{n}}\frac{1-q^{2n}u}{1-u}
. Then, for
0\leq n\leq\theta
:

\displaystyle C_{n}^{(q,q^{\theta})}\big(x_{1}/\big(q^{\theta}x_{2}\big)\big)=\frac{q^{\theta}x_{2}-q^{2n}x_{1}}{q^{\theta}x_{2}-x_{1}}\frac{1}{t^{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{n}{\left\{\frac{q^{\theta}-q^{i-1}}{1-q^{i}}\frac{q^{\theta}x_{2}-q^{i-1}x_{1}}{x_{2}-q^{i}x_{1}}\right\}}.

(B.2)

From (
B.2
), it is only a matter of tedious computation to check the three identities given in the lemma.
∎
